Program Title:  Instrumentation and Regulation Critical Spares Program 
 
Spending Rationale: 
 
  Mandated 
  Customer Connections 
  Reliability 
Non-Infrastructure
 
Brief Description: 
 
There are numerous specialty pieces of equipment and materials required to maintain gas 
regulating stations and metering sites.  Unfortunately, many of these materials are non-
standard and non-stock items.  It is not unheard of to wait four months before delivery of 
some of these materials.  If a long lead time part within a gas regulating station were to 
fail, it could take weeks, if not months, to receive replacement parts.  This program 
would create a special store room designed to stock some of the non-standard, long lead 
time items. 
 
 
 
Program Justification: 
 
From time to time, the Company encounters issues within gas regulating stations and at 
customer metering sites.  In many cases such issues can be resolved by breaking down 
the parts, cleaning them, and rebuilding them.  In some cases; however, rebuilding is 
insufficient and replacement is required.  The Company does not maintain spare parts for 
every item within a regulating station.  Sometimes, replacement parts and materials take 
weeks to receive.  This could jeopardize the Company’s ability to continuously provide 
safe and reliable gas service. 
 
The goal of this program is to identify all the critical items within a gas regulating station 
and at customer metering sites.  These items will be cataloged and prioritized as to their 
criticality and availability from the vendors who sell them.  Orders will be placed for the 
items that rank the highest.  Once received these items will be stored within a special 
room and an employee will be assigned to maintain the room and materials.   
 
The goal is to retain one critical spare for the following types of equipment: regulators, 
relief valves, chart boxes, filters, heater components, valves, rebuild kits, pressure and 
temperature transmitters, components for chromatographs, moisture analyzers, remote-
controlled valves (“RCVs”), ultra-sonic meters (“USMs”), regulator pilots, remote 
transmitter units (“RTUs”), specialty tooling, odorizer system components, and generator 
parts.  The total cost to stock the necessary items is estimated to be $1,050,000.

Program Title:  Oil Seal Replacement Program 
 
Spending Rationale: 
 
  Mandated 
  Customer Connections 
  Reliability 
Non-Infrastructure
 
Brief Description: 
 
Oil Seals have been used for years as a form of over pressure protection at low-pressure 
regulating stations.  When installed, they were a very good option for protecting low-pressure 
distribution systems in the event of a regulator failure.  Over time though, new technology has 
produced different products that are more precise and are friendlier to the environment.  This 
program proposes replacing all aging oil seals with a mechanical relief valve over a five-year 
period. 
 
Justification: 
 
The Company utilizes 48 oil seals at 41 different locations to protect its low-pressure distribution 
systems.  Oil seals were used extensively up to the 1970s, but eventually superseded by 
mechanical relief valve devices.  Oil seals were typically homemade devices that would utilize 
the weight of oil to hold back low-pressure gas.  If the gas pressure were to increase, then the gas 
would push through the oil and relieve to atmosphere.  In many cases, when the gas pressure is 
relieved the oil is blown to the atmosphere as well.  Some of the oil seals were made with a 
special canister that would try to capture the oil, but in any event at least some oil would be 
released to the environment. 
 
There are a couple of downfalls in respect to an oil seal.  As described earlier, there are 
environmental issues that can occur if an oil seal relives.  It is also difficult to get the oil into the 
seal without spilling the oil on the ground.  However, the biggest issue is in the operation of the 
device.  Once lifted the oil seal will continue to vent gas even if the pressure spike subsides.  It is 
only when the device is valved off that the gas venting stops.  The newer technology will reseat 
and stop the flow of gas once the pressure reaches a safe point.  Regulator failures often are not 
catastrophic, meaning only small amounts of gas will pass by.   In this type of scenario, 
mechanical devices may open only for a few seconds and then close again.  However, an oil seal 
will not reseat and will continue to vent large amounts of gas. 
 
The Company estimates it will cost $167,000 to replace each oil seal.  There are seven stations 
that contain two oil seals and there could be efficiencies gained for those locations.  The work 
would include retiring the old oil seal, installing a new vault, and installing the new mechanical 
relief device with the associated piping.

Additional benefits include enhanced ability by National Grid to respond to and manage 
emergency situations including equipment malfunctions, enhanced employee safety, and 
enhanced public and customer safety.  The functionality of the new equipment will help 
maintain an environmentally safe area and help reduce carbon emissions in the event of a 
regulator failure.  Lastly, replacing aging infrastructure will allow for safer gas regulating 
station operations.

Program Title: Smart Residential Methane Detector Program 
 
Spending Rationale: 
 
  Mandated 
  Customer Connections 
  Reliability 
  Non-Infrastructure
 
Brief Description: 
This program funds the design, development, implementation and maintenance of a Smart 
Residential Methane Detector (RMD) program.  The Smart RMD will be UL-approved and 
utilize the latest technologies tested by the Company’s material lab.  The Smart RMD will house 
a telecommunication module that will allow data transfer between the Company and the Smart 
RMD.  The Smart RMD real-time data transfer will leverage the telecommunication network 
being developed as part of the Advanced Meter Infrastructure (AMI) program.  Program costs 
include information system modification, equipment purchase, equipment installation and project 
management. 
Pilot Scope Breakdown

Program Justification: 
The primary drivers for the Smart RMD pilot program are increased customer safety and a more 
focused emergency response in the event of a gas leak.  Leveraging the telecommunication 
solution implemented as part of the AMI project will provide the Smart RMD pilot with the 
necessary data paths and connections from the customer, through the head end systems, to the 
Company.  Smart RMD’s will contribute to the future ecosystem of communicating gas devices 
to increase the safety and reliability for customers.

Program Title: Smart Gas Meter AMI Integration 
 
Spending Rationale: 
 
  Mandated 
  Customer Connections 
  Reliability 
  Non-Infrastructure
 
Brief Description: 
This program funds the design, integration and deployment of 400 smart gas meters in support of 
the Advanced Meter Infrastructure (AMI) initiative.  A smart gas meter measures gas usage and 
provides additional safety functionality including over-pressure sensing, over-temperature 
sensing, excess flow detection, advanced air/theft detection and an integrated shutoff valve.  The 
integrated shutoff valve can be triggered if any of the stated conditions are detected.  The same 
valve can be proactively deployed for other safety situations such as soft-offs, gas leaks or gas 
emergencies.  The smart gas meters measure gas using ultrasonic technology and are battery 
operated with a 20-year expected lifetime.  The gas communication module is housed within the 
shell of the smart meter.  
Several manufacturers produce smart gas meters and this program will fund meter purchases from 
each of these manufacturers.  The full safety benefit of smart gas meters requires a form of real-
time communication to alert the Company in the event that one of the additional functionalities is 
triggered.  The AMI program deploys a complete end-to-end solution that connects the customer 
to the Company directly through a telecommunications network.  The smart gas meter can operate 
in a non-AMI solution but any safety event will not be known to the Company until monthly meter 
readings are collected or a customer-initiated response is required.  A fully enabled smart gas meter 
solution not only gives real-time notifications if there is an abnormal operating conditions but also 
enables a more focused emergency response.  
 
Program Scope Breakdown 
- 
FY22 
o Evaluate Smart Gas Meter supplier options 
- 
FY23 
o Information System Requirements Identification, Design and Development 
o Smart Gas Meter Material Testing and Communication Testing 
- 
FY24 
o Information System Implementation 
o Smart Gas Meter Equipment Purchase 
o Smart Gas Meter Equipment Installation 
Exhibit__(GIOP-4) 
Page 128 of 136


--- Page 246 ---
 
Program Justification: 
The primary drivers for the Smart Gas Meter Program are: increased customer and workforce 
safety, real-time feedback of potential gas safety events, and a more focused emergency response.  
Leveraging the telecommunication solution implemented as part of the AMI project will provide 
the Smart Gas Meters with the necessary data paths and connections from the customer, through 
the head end systems, to the Company.  Smart Gas Meters will be a contribution to the future 
ecosystem of communicating gas devices to increase the safety and reliability to the Company’s 
gas customers.

Program Title: Low-Pressure Zone Smart Meter Program  
 
Spending Rationale: 
 
  Mandated 
  Customer Connections 
  Reliability 
  Non-Infrastructure
 
Brief Description: 
This program funds the purchase, deployment, and integration of Smart Gas Meters on services in 
low pressure zones.  The Company has 76,061 customers connected to low pressure services.  Low 
pressure customers do not possess the additional layer-of-protection that a service regulator 
provides to high-pressure customers.  This program targets customers the Company expects to be 
on a low pressure system for the foreseeable future.  A Smart Gas Meter measures gas usage and 
provides additional safety functionality including over-pressure sensing, over-temperature 
sensing, excess flow detection, advanced air/theft detection and an integrated shutoff valve.  The 
integrated shutoff valve can be triggered if any of the stated conditions are detected. 
Smart Gas Meters connect to a service line through the same hub design as traditional diaphragm 
meters and there is no incremental workload to install a Smart Gas Meter.  Smart Gas Meters will 
be deployed proactively through the Company’s meter change program.  Deployment of smart 
meters will require minor Information Technology changes to be able to receive the safety alarms 
and events that a gas smart meter will communicate through hand held devices. 
The same valve can be proactively deployed for other safety situations such as soft-offs, gas leaks, 
or gas emergencies through the use of a handheld device.  The Gas Smart Meters measure gas 
using ultrasonic technology and are battery operated with a 20-year expected lifetime.  The gas 
communication module is housed within the shell of the smart meter.  The number of smart meters 
required to support the program for FY22-FY25 is estimated in the table below:

This option does not increase safety or reliability to the customers

Program Title: Purchase of Miscellaneous Capital Tools & Equipment 
 
Spending Rationale: 
 
  Mandated 
  Customer Connections 
  Reliability 
  Non-Infrastructure
 
Brief Description: 
 
This program funds purchases of tools and equipment not dedicated to specific projects.  These 
items support the safety of employees, customers and the general public.  The items provide cost 
efficiencies across multiple mandated programs and commitments to customer needs and 
expectations, and allow for potential increase in productivity for on-going day-to-day operations.  
 
This program typically supports process-related initiatives to achieve the goals of the entire gas 
organization.  These items relate to safety (e.g., mechanized maintenance of traffic devices, 
worker safety enhancements, etc.), climate change (e.g., apparatus to minimize methane 
emissions through natural gas drawdown operations), support of new, emerging and on-going 
technologies (e.g., capital spares and parts for trenchless and keyhole technologies) and the 
initiation of innovative applications that will lead to improved operations. 
Program Justification:  
Current Company policy capitalizes general tool and/or equipment purchases subject to a 
predetermined minimum dollar thresholds ($2,500).  Such general equipment includes tooling 
(hand, power, pneumatic, hydraulic, etc.).  The Purchase Miscellaneous Capital Tools and 
Equipment line item captures the above-mentioned items that are not used for specific projects 
but rather support the safe, efficient and on-going day-to-day operations of the gas business unit.  
Purchase of Miscellaneous Capital Tools and Equipment utilize project numbers that are 
budgeted based on historical funding due to the inability to associate this equipment with any 
one specific project. 
 
Program Budget Methodology:  
 
The Purchase of Miscellaneous Capital Tools and Equipment program reflects historical 
spending adjusted for inflation and for increases in mandated program work, for example, leak 
prone pipe replacement.  The volume of public safety equipment, road traffic plates and sheeting 
systems are just a few examples of additional tools and equipment needed to support mandated 
program increases. 
 
In addition, FY24 has an increase in spend to fund the needed replacement of the combustible 
gas indicator (CGI) inventory that is reaching the end of its useful life.  This population includes 
700 CGI’s at $3,107/unit. 
 
 
 
Exhibit__(GIOP-4) 
Page 132 of 136

Customer Benefit:   
• Improved public safety due to mechanized maintenance of traffic devices and public safety 
enhancements.  
• Noise reduction enhancements with new technology tooling. 
• Productivity increases and potential unit cost reductions. 
• Compliance with federal and state code requirements including new US Department of 
Transportation (USDOT) regulations. 
• Reduction of methane emissions and reduction of greenhouse gases. 
 
Alternatives:  
 
Alternative 1: Reduce Request  
 
Reducing the budget line item is not recommended because the tools purchased through this 
program drive process changes that support new initiatives and productivity improvements 
throughout the Gas distribution organization.  Reduced funding for this program could drive a 
downturn in safety for the company, employees, customers and general public. 
 
Alternative 2: Do Nothing  
 
Removal of this program would require spending for required tools to be allocated to specific 
projects and mandated programs resulting in inconsistent unit costs, excessive tool ordering (lack 
of controls) and could jeopardize safety for the company, employees, customers and general 
public.

--- Page 251 ---
 
 
Program Title:  Learning and Development - Materials, Tools, and Equipment 
 
Spending Rationale: 
 
  Mandated 
  Growth 
  Reliability 
Non-Infrastructure
 
 
Brief Description: 
 
This program proposes $1.0M of incremental CapEx in FY22 – FY25 for tool upgrades, 
new tools and equipment, equipment maintenance, replacement of worn or defective 
equipment, as well as training resources and materials to support new gas technical  
training and field training/evaluation.  
 
 
Justification: 
 
In order to conform to new requirements and increasing demands of the industry, 
Learning and Development (“L&D”)’s Gas Technical Training (GTT) is in need of 
increasing, enhancing and improving its training resources.  L&D has not previously been 
afforded a budget or allowance for the purchase of new products, materials, tools or 
equipment or resources to perform the necessary maintenance on existing tools, 
equipment and training props.  GTT always sought assistance from the lines of business it 
serves but resources have become limited and minimal.  This proposal will allow GTT to 
train and have employees demonstrate at a standard rate and volume necessary for the 
safe operation of its employees and Contractors on our infrastructure.   
 
 
This proposal will allow for the purchase of new tools and equipment at the beginning of 
the Annual Expert Training season.  This is required due to the number of employees 
trained and qualified during the season which far exceeds L&D’s existing resources.  The 
primary need for tools and equipment is for instruction on capital construction standards 
procedures and installation methods as well as mandated/maintenance activities.  
 
 
This proposal will allow L&D to continually train and qualify existing employees and 
new hires throughout the year.  This also allows employees to utilize new, state of the art 
equipment which they have been directly trained on for actual capital construction and 
maintenance activities. 
 
This proposal will ensure the proper knowledge and use of up to date tools and 
equipment, the proper application of procedures used, and all personnel are operating in a 
safe, competent and efficient manner.  It also ensures all employees are complying with 
the most recent procedure updates, Technical Bulletins and NYSPSC Order 
Requirements as amended in the NYSCRR and provided through our Operating 
Procedures.

Program Title: Meter Testing Equipment 
 
Spending Rationale: 
 
  Mandated 
 
  Customer Connections 
  Reliability 
  Non-Infrastructure
 
Brief Description: 
This program funds the purchase of meter testing equipment to support the testing of gas meters 
for accuracy and leaks and the replacement of meter lab equipment that has reached its end of 
life so as to ensure continued safety, compliance and meter testing accuracy.  State regulations 
require random sampling of gas meter performance on an annual basis.  Meters are classified 
based on manufacture/model, and the number of meters to be tested within each of these 
classifications is determined by the population size.  Accuracy and leak testing of the gas meters 
is performed at the Company meter lab. 
Program Justification: 
The primary driver for meter testing equipment purchases is compliance with state regulations 
governing gas meter testing accuracy and the continued support of meter testing programs.

Notes and Assumptions:
1) LPP replacement capital expenditures in base rates total $67.368 million (46.13 miles) in RYE Jun22, $78.058 million (50 miles) in RYE Jun23, $81.861 million (50 miles) in RYE Jun24.
Revenue requirement assumes capital expenditures are placed in-service in the month spent.
2) Depreciation is based on the composite depreciation rate for mains and services based on depreciation study recommendations.
3) Utilizes pre-tax WACC by fiscal year per filing with a 9.5% ROE.
4) The amount subject to surcharge is limited to the Company's average costs of main replacement allowed in rate allowance, subject to a 2% cap
5) The RYE Jun22 incremental mileage spend will be rolled forward to the RYE Jun23 and RYE Jun24 surcharges, calculating a return on the net plant investment and associated depreciation expense.
6) The RYE Jun23 incremental mileage spend will be rolled forward to the RYE Jun24,  calculating a return on the net plant investment and associated depreciation expense.
